\begin{split}\max_{L=0,\ldots,K_{\max}}\left|\sum_{l=0}^{L}{\left\{\left(\varepsilon^{E}_{\hat{\tau}^{O}_{L,l+1}}\right)^{2}-\mathbb{E}\left[\left.\left(\varepsilon^{E}_{\hat{\tau}^{O}_{L,l+1}}\right)^{2}\right|\mathcal{E}^{O}\right]\right\}}\right|=\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}}\left(\left(K_{\max}\log K_{\max}\right)^{1/2}\,\overline{\sigma}^{2}\right).\end{split}

Consider the last term on the r.h.s. in (
88
). Each conditional expectation is bounded by
\overline{\sigma}^{2}
almost surely. Moreover, the conditional expectations can only differ if there is a true change-point between
\hat{\tau}^{O}_{L,l}
and
\hat{\tau}^{O}_{L,l+1}
as observations on the same segment have the same variance-covariance matrix. Hence, at most
K
terms can be non-zero. Thus almost surely

\max_{L=0,\ldots,K_{\max}}\sum_{l=0}^{L}{\left|\mathbb{E}\left[\left.\left(\varepsilon^{E}_{\hat{\tau}^{O}_{L,l+1}}\right)^{2}\right|\mathcal{E}^{O}\right]-\mathbb{E}\left[\left.\frac{1}{\hat{n}^{O}_{l}-1}\sum_{i=\hat{\tau}^{O}_{L,l}+1}^{\hat{\tau}^{O}_{L,l+1}-1}{\left(\varepsilon^{E}_{i}\right)^{2}}\right|\mathcal{E}^{O}\right]\right|}\leq K\overline{\sigma}^{2}.

Hence,

\max_{L=0,\ldots,K_{\max}}A_{11,L}^{(n)}=\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}}\left(\left(K_{\max}\log K_{\max}\right)^{1/2}\,\overline{\sigma}^{2}\right).

Recall that we have assumed
\left(K_{\max}\log K_{\max}\right)^{1/2}=o(\log\log\overline{\lambda})
, see Assumption
1
(iii)
. Thus putting things together, we see that

\max_{L=0,\ldots,K_{\max}}A_{11,L}^{(n)}=o_{\mathbb{P}}(\overline{\sigma}^{2}\log\log\overline{\lambda}).

(89)

Bounding
A_{12,L}^{(n)}

We now bound the second term in the r.h.s. of (
87
):

\displaystyle A_{12,L}^{(n)}=

\displaystyle\left|\sum_{l=0}^{L}{\overline{\varepsilon}^{O}_{\hat{\tau}^{O}_{L,l}:\hat{\tau}^{O}_{L,l+1}}\left(\frac{1}{\hat{n}^{O}_{l}-1}\sum_{i=\hat{\tau}^{O}_{L,l}+1}^{\hat{\tau}^{O}_{L,l+1}-1}{\varepsilon^{E}_{i}}-\varepsilon^{E}_{\hat{\tau}^{O}_{L,l+1}}\right)}\right|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left|\sum_{l=0}^{L}{\overline{\varepsilon}^{O}_{\hat{\tau}^{O}_{L,l}:\hat{\tau}^{O}_{L,l+1}}\frac{1}{\hat{n}^{O}_{l}-1}\sum_{i=\hat{\tau}^{O}_{L,l}+1}^{\hat{\tau}^{O}_{L,l+1}-1}{\varepsilon^{E}_{i}}}\right|+\left|\sum_{l=0}^{L}{\overline{\varepsilon}^{O}_{\hat{\tau}^{O}_{L,l}:\hat{\tau}^{O}_{L,l+1}}\varepsilon^{E}_{\hat{\tau}^{O}_{L,l+1}}}\right|.

(90)

We focus on the second term in the display above, but the same steps lead to the same bound for the first term. It follows from the law of total probability that for any
\mathcal{E}^{0}
-measurable random variable
X
that is positive almost surely,

\begin{split}&\mathbb{P}\left(\left|\sum_{l=0}^{L}{\overline{\varepsilon}^{O}_{\hat{\tau}^{O}_{L,l}:\hat{\tau}^{O}_{L,l+1}}\varepsilon^{E}_{\hat{\tau}^{O}_{L,l+1}}}\right|\geq X\right)\\
=&\mathbb{P}\left(\sum_{l=0}^{L}{\overline{\varepsilon}^{O}_{\hat{\tau}^{O}_{L,l}:\hat{\tau}^{O}_{L,l+1}}\varepsilon^{E}_{\hat{\tau}^{O}_{L,l+1}}}\geq X\right)+\mathbb{P}\left(\sum_{l=0}^{L}{\overline{\varepsilon}^{O}_{\hat{\tau}^{O}_{L,l}:\hat{\tau}^{O}_{L,l+1}}\varepsilon^{E}_{\hat{\tau}^{O}_{L,l+1}}}\leq-X\right)\\
=&\mathbb{E}\left[\mathbb{P}\left(\left.\sum_{l=0}^{L}{\overline{\varepsilon}^{O}_{\hat{\tau}^{O}_{L,l}:\hat{\tau}^{O}_{L,l+1}}\varepsilon^{E}_{\hat{\tau}^{O}_{L,l+1}}}\geq X\right|\mathcal{E}^{O}\right)\right]+\mathbb{E}\left[\mathbb{P}\left(\left.\sum_{l=0}^{L}{\overline{\varepsilon}^{O}_{\hat{\tau}^{O}_{L,l}:\hat{\tau}^{O}_{L,l+1}}\varepsilon^{E}_{\hat{\tau}^{O}_{L,l+1}}}\leq-X\right|\mathcal{E}^{O}\right)\right].\end{split}

We focus on the first term and the probability inside the expectation. The second term can be bounded in the same way. Let
L=0,\ldots,K_{\max}
be fixed. We will use Bernstein’s inequality conditional on
\mathcal{E}^{O}
. To this end, note that
\overline{\varepsilon}^{O}_{\hat{\tau}^{O}_{L,l}:\hat{\tau}^{O}_{L,l+1}}\varepsilon^{E}_{\hat{\tau}^{O}_{L,l+1}},\ l=0,\ldots,L
, are independent conditional on
\mathcal{E}^{O}

with

\mathbb{E}\Big[\left(\overline{\varepsilon}^{O}_{\hat{\tau}^{O}_{L,l}:\hat{\tau}^{O}_{L,l+1}}\right)^{\top}\varepsilon^{E}_{\hat{\tau}^{O}_{L,l+1}}\Big|\mathcal{E}^{O}\Big]=0,\quad\forall\ l=0,\ldots,L.

Moreover, it follows from the Cauchy–Schwarz inequality and Assumption
2
that

\displaystyle\sum_{l=0}^{L}\mathbb{E}\bigg[\bigg(\left(\overline{\varepsilon}^{O}_{\hat{\tau}^{O}_{L,l}:\hat{\tau}^{O}_{L,l+1}}\right)^{\top}\varepsilon^{E}_{\hat{\tau}^{O}_{L,l+1}}\bigg)^{2}\bigg|\mathcal{E}^{O}\bigg]\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{l=0}^{L}\mathbb{E}\bigg[\left\|\overline{\varepsilon}^{O}_{\hat{\tau}^{O}_{L,l}:\hat{\tau}^{O}_{L,l+1}}\right\|_{2}^{2}\left\|\varepsilon^{E}_{\hat{\tau}^{O}_{L,l+1}}\right\|_{2}^{2}\bigg|\mathcal{E}^{O}\bigg]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\overline{\sigma}^{2}\sum_{l=0}^{L}\left\|\overline{\varepsilon}^{O}_{\hat{\tau}^{O}_{L,l}:\hat{\tau}^{O}_{L,l+1}}\right\|_{2}^{2},

\displaystyle\sum_{l=0}^{L}\mathbb{E}\bigg[\bigg(\left(\overline{\varepsilon}^{O}_{\hat{\tau}^{O}_{L,l}:\hat{\tau}^{O}_{L,l+1}}\right)^{\top}\varepsilon^{E}_{\hat{\tau}^{O}_{L,l+1}}\bigg)^{q}\bigg|\mathcal{E}^{O}\bigg]\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{l=0}^{L}\mathbb{E}\bigg[\left\|\overline{\varepsilon}^{O}_{\hat{\tau}^{O}_{L,l}:\hat{\tau}^{O}_{L,l+1}}\right\|_{2}^{q}\left\|\varepsilon^{E}_{\hat{\tau}^{O}_{L,l+1}}\right\|_{2}^{q}\bigg|\mathcal{E}^{O}\bigg]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{q!}{2}c^{q-2}\overline{\sigma}^{q}\sum_{l=0}^{L}\left\|\overline{\varepsilon}^{O}_{\hat{\tau}^{O}_{L,l}:\hat{\tau}^{O}_{L,l+1}}\right\|_{2}^{q}\leq

\displaystyle\frac{q!}{2}\overline{\sigma}^{2}\sum_{l=0}^{L}\left\|\overline{\varepsilon}^{O}_{\hat{\tau}^{O}_{L,l}:\hat{\tau}^{O}_{L,l+1}}\right\|_{2}^{2}\left(c\,\overline{\sigma}\left(\sum_{l=0}^{L}\left\|\overline{\varepsilon}^{O}_{\hat{\tau}^{O}_{L,l}:\hat{\tau}^{O}_{L,l+1}}\right\|_{2}^{2}\right)^{1/2}\right)^{q-2},

for
q\geq 3
. Thus, it follows from Bernstein’s inequality
(
boucheron2013concentration
, Cor. 2.11)
that for any
a\geq 1

\begin{split}&\mathbb{P}\left(\left.\sum_{l=0}^{L}{\overline{\varepsilon}^{O}_{\hat{\tau}^{O}_{L,l}:\hat{\tau}^{O}_{L,l+1}}\varepsilon^{E}_{\hat{\tau}^{O}_{L,l+1}}}\geq a\log\left(K_{\max}\right)\overline{\sigma}\left(\sum_{l=0}^{L}\left(\overline{\varepsilon}^{O}_{\hat{\tau}^{O}_{L,l}:\hat{\tau}^{O}_{L,l+1}}\right)^{2}\right)^{1/2}(\sqrt{2}+c)\right|\mathcal{E}^{O}\right)\\
\leq&\exp(-a\log\left(K_{\max}\right))=K_{\max}^{-a}.\end{split}

Hence, by a similar argument involving the negative, we have

\begin{split}\mathbb{P}\left(\left|\sum_{l=0}^{L}{\overline{\varepsilon}^{O}_{\hat{\tau}^{O}_{L,l}:\hat{\tau}^{O}_{L,l+1}}\varepsilon^{E}_{\hat{\tau}^{O}_{L,l+1}}}\right|\geq a\log\left(K_{\max}\right)\overline{\sigma}\left(\sum_{l=0}^{L}\left(\overline{\varepsilon}^{O}_{\hat{\tau}^{O}_{L,l}:\hat{\tau}^{O}_{L,l+1}}\right)^{2}\right)^{1/2}(\sqrt{2}+c)\right)\leq 2K_{\max}^{-a}\end{split}

and so

\begin{split}&\max_{L=0,\ldots,K_{\max}}\left(\sum_{l=0}^{L}\left(\overline{\varepsilon}^{O}_{\hat{\tau}^{O}_{L,l}:\hat{\tau}^{O}_{L,l+1}}\right)^{2}\right)^{-1/2}\left|\sum_{l=0}^{L}{\overline{\varepsilon}^{O}_{\hat{\tau}^{O}_{L,l}:\hat{\tau}^{O}_{L,l+1}}\varepsilon^{E}_{\hat{\tau}^{O}_{L,l+1}}}\right|=\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}}\left(\log\left(K_{\max}\right)\overline{\sigma}\right).\end{split}

As remarked, the same bound applies to the first term in (
90
). Hence, we obtain

\begin{split}&\max_{L=0,\ldots,K_{\max}}\left(\sum_{l=0}^{L}\left(\overline{\varepsilon}^{O}_{\hat{\tau}^{O}_{L,l}:\hat{\tau}^{O}_{L,l+1}}\right)^{2}\right)^{-1/2}A_{12,L}^{(n)}=\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}}\left(\log\left(K_{\max}\right)\overline{\sigma}\right).\end{split}

(91)

We now simplify this term. To this end, we use the notation

\left\{\tilde{\tau}^{O}_{L,0},\ldots,\tilde{\tau}^{O}_{L,\tilde{L}+1}\right\}:=\left\{\tau_{0}^{O},\ldots,\tau_{K+1}^{O}\right\}\,\cup\,\left\{\hat{\tau}^{O}_{L,1},\ldots,\hat{\tau}^{O}_{L,L}\right\},

with
\tilde{L}\leq K+L
. Further, we use the fact that
(\sum_{i=1}^{n}a_{i})^{2}\leq\sum_{i=1}^{n}na_{i}^{2}
for any
a_{1},\ldots,a_{n}\in\mathbb{R}
. Using this and that the number of change-points in the set above between
\hat{\tau}^{O}_{L,l}
and
\hat{\tau}^{O}_{L,l+1}
can be upper bounded by the number of observations
\tilde{\tau}^{O}_{L,l+1}-\tilde{\tau}^{O}_{L,l}
, we have

\begin{split}\sum_{l=0}^{L}{\left(\overline{\varepsilon}^{O}_{\hat{\tau}^{O}_{L,l}:\hat{\tau}^{O}_{L,l+1}}\right)^{2}}&\leq\sum_{l=0}^{\tilde{L}}{\left(\tilde{\tau}^{O}_{L,l+1}-\tilde{\tau}^{O}_{L,l}\right)\left(\overline{\varepsilon}^{O}_{\tilde{\tau}^{O}_{L,l}:\tilde{\tau}^{O}_{L,l+1}}\right)^{2}}\\
=&\sum_{k=0}^{K}{\sum_{i=\tau^{O}_{k}+1}^{\tau^{O}_{k+1}}{\left(\varepsilon^{O}_{i}-\overline{\varepsilon}^{O}_{\tau^{O}_{k}:\tau^{O}_{k+1}}\right)^{2}}}-\sum_{l=0}^{\tilde{L}}{\sum_{i=\tilde{\tau}^{O}_{L,l}+1}^{\tilde{\tau}^{O}_{L,l+1}}{\left(\varepsilon^{O}_{i}-\overline{\varepsilon}^{O}_{\tilde{\tau}^{O}_{L,l}:\tilde{\tau}^{O}_{L,l+1}}\right)^{2}}}\\
&+\sum_{k=0}^{K}{\left(\tau^{O}_{k+1}-\tau^{O}_{k}\right)\left(\overline{\varepsilon}^{O}_{\tau^{O}_{k}:\tau^{O}_{k+1}}\right)^{2}}\\
=&S_{\varepsilon^{O}}\left(\mathcal{T}^{O}_{K}\right)-S_{\varepsilon^{O}}\left(\hat{\mathcal{T}}^{O}_{L}\cup\mathcal{T}^{O}_{K}\right)+\sum_{k=0}^{K}{\left(\tau^{O}_{k+1}-\tau^{O}_{k}\right)\left(\overline{\varepsilon}^{O}_{\tau^{O}_{k}:\tau^{O}_{k+1}}\right)^{2}}.\end{split}

(92)

Note that by Markov’s inequality,

\sum_{k=0}^{K}{\left(\tau^{O}_{k+1}-\tau^{O}_{k}\right)\left(\overline{\varepsilon}^{O}_{\tau^{O}_{k}:\tau^{O}_{k+1}}\right)^{2}}=\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}}\left((K\vee 1)\overline{\sigma}^{2}\right).

(93)

To bound the terms further we distinguish the cases
L=K,L>K,
and
L<K
. We will also combine the obtained bounds with (
89
) to obtain the final statement for
A_{1,L}^{(n)}
.

Case
L=K
.

From Lemma
13
(ii)
we have that

S_{\varepsilon^{O}}\left(\mathcal{T}^{O}_{K}\right)-S_{\varepsilon^{O}}\left(\hat{\mathcal{T}}^{O}_{K}\cup\mathcal{T}^{O}_{K}\right)=o_{\mathbb{P}}\left(\overline{\sigma}^{2}\log\log\overline{\lambda}\right).

Moreover, Assumption
1
(ii)
yields
K=o(\log\log\overline{\lambda})
. Thus,

\sum_{k=0}^{K}{\left(\overline{\varepsilon}^{O}_{\hat{\tau}^{O}_{K,k}:\hat{\tau}^{O}_{K,k+1}}\right)^{2}}=o_{\mathbb{P}}(\overline{\sigma}^{2}\log\log\overline{\lambda}),

(94)

and this also holds when
K=0
. Furthermore, it follows from Assumption
1
(iii)
that
\log K_{\max}=o\Big((\log\log\overline{\lambda})^{1/2}\Big)
. To this end, note that this is trivially satisfied if
K_{\max}=\mathcal{O}(1)
and otherwise since
(\log K_{\max})^{3}/K_{\max}\to 0
.
Hence,

\begin{split}\left|\sum_{k=0}^{K}{\overline{\varepsilon}^{O}_{\hat{\tau}^{O}_{K,k}:\hat{\tau}^{O}_{K,k+1}}\varepsilon^{E}_{\hat{\tau}^{O}_{K,k+1}}}\right|=o_{\mathbb{P}}(\overline{\sigma}^{2}\log\log\overline{\lambda})\end{split}

and thus using (
89
) we have

A_{1,K}^{(n)}=o_{\mathbb{P}}(\overline{\sigma}^{2}\log\log\overline{\lambda}).

(95)

We will use this bound in the following to bound
\max\big(A_{1,L}^{(n)},\,A_{1,K}^{(n)}\big)
for
L>K
and
L<K
.

Case
L>K
.

Using (
92
), (
93
), Assumptions
1
(ii)
and
(iii)
similarly to the case where
L=K
as well as Assumption
4
gives us

\max_{L=K+1,\ldots,K_{\max}}\left(S_{\varepsilon^{O}}\left(\mathcal{T}^{O}_{K}\right)-S_{\varepsilon^{O}}\left(\hat{\mathcal{T}}^{O}_{L}\cup\mathcal{T}^{O}_{K}\right)\right)^{-1}\sum_{l=0}^{L}{\left(\overline{\varepsilon}^{O}_{\hat{\tau}^{O}_{L,l}:\hat{\tau}^{O}_{L,l+1}}\right)^{2}}=\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}}(1).

(96)

Combining this with (
89
) and (
91
) as well as (
95
) and Assumption
4
yields

\begin{split}&\max_{L=K+1,\ldots,K_{\max}}\left(S_{\varepsilon^{O}}\left(\mathcal{T}^{O}_{K}\right)-S_{\varepsilon^{O}}\left(\hat{\mathcal{T}}^{O}_{L}\cup\mathcal{T}^{O}_{K}\right)\right)^{-1}\max\big(A_{1,L}^{(n)},\,A_{1,K}^{(n)}\big)=o_{\mathbb{P}}(1).\end{split}

Note that this completes the proof in the case where
K=0\ \forall\ n
as we only have to consider
L\geq K
and since all
\mu_{i}
’s are the same, we have that
A_{L}^{(n)}\leq A_{1,L}^{(n)}
. Moreover, note that the arguments above in the case where
K=0
do not use Assumptions
3
and
5
. In the following we may therefore assume that
K\geq 1
.

Case
L<K
.
From Lemma
13
(i)
we have that

\max_{L=0\ldots,K-1}\left\{S_{\varepsilon^{O}}\left(\mathcal{T}^{O}_{K}\right)-S_{\varepsilon^{O}}\left(\hat{\mathcal{T}}^{O}_{L}\cup\mathcal{T}^{O}_{K}\right)\right\}=\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}}\big(K\overline{\sigma}^{2}(\log\overline{\lambda})^{2}\big).

Thus, (
92
) and (
93
) yields

\max_{L=0\ldots,K-1}\sum_{l=0}^{L}{\left(\overline{\varepsilon}^{O}_{\hat{\tau}^{O}_{L,l}:\hat{\tau}^{O}_{L,l+1}}\right)^{2}}=\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}}\big(K\overline{\sigma}^{2}(\log\overline{\lambda})^{2}\big).

(97)

Consequently, (
91
) and (
95
) give us

\begin{split}\max_{L=0\ldots,K-1}\max\big(A_{12,L}^{(n)},\,A_{12,K}^{(n)}\big)=\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}}\big(K\overline{\sigma}^{2}(\log\overline{\lambda})^{2}\big)\end{split}

and hence from (
89
) we have that

\begin{split}\max_{L=0\ldots,K-1}\max\big(A_{1,L}^{(n)},\,A_{1,K}^{(n)}\big)=\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}}\big(K\overline{\sigma}^{2}(\log\overline{\lambda})^{2}\big).\end{split}

Finally, note that
\mathcal{I}_{L}
is non-empty. Hence,
\sum_{k\in\mathcal{I}_{L}}{\Delta_{k}^{2}}\geq\Delta_{(1)}^{2}
. Thus, Assumption
5
yields

K\overline{\sigma}^{2}(\log\overline{\lambda})^{2}=o_{\mathbb{P}}\left(\min_{L}\underline{\lambda}\sum_{k\in\mathcal{I}_{L}}{\Delta_{k}^{2}}\right).

In summary, it follows that
A_{1,L}^{(n)}
satisfies the same bounds as
A_{L}^{(n)}
in the statement of the lemma.

Bounding
A_{2,L}^{(n)}

\begin{split}A_{2,L}^{(n)}\leq&\left|\sum_{l=0}^{L}\sum_{i=\hat{\tau}^{O}_{L,l}+1}^{\hat{\tau}^{O}_{L,l+1}-1}{\frac{\hat{n}^{O}_{l}}{\hat{n}^{O}_{l}-1}\left(\mu^{E}_{i}-\overline{\mu}^{E}_{\hat{\tau}^{O}_{L,l}:\hat{\tau}^{O}_{L,l+1}-1}\right)^{2}}-\sum_{l=0}^{L}\sum_{i=\hat{\tau}^{O}_{L,l}+1}^{\hat{\tau}^{O}_{L,l+1}-1}{\frac{\hat{n}^{O}_{l}}{\hat{n}^{O}_{l}-1}\left(\mu^{E}_{i}-\overline{\mu}^{O}_{\hat{\tau}^{O}_{L,l}:\hat{\tau}^{O}_{L,l+1}}\right)^{2}}\right|\\
&+\left|\sum_{l=0}^{L}\sum_{i=\hat{\tau}^{O}_{L,l}+1}^{\hat{\tau}^{O}_{L,l+1}-1}{\frac{\hat{n}^{O}_{l}}{\hat{n}^{O}_{l}-1}\left(\mu^{E}_{i}-\overline{\mu}^{O}_{\hat{\tau}^{O}_{L,l}:\hat{\tau}^{O}_{L,l+1}}\right)^{2}}-\sum_{l=0}^{L}\sum_{i=\hat{\tau}^{O}_{L,l}+1}^{\hat{\tau}^{O}_{L,l+1}-1}{\frac{\hat{n}^{O}_{l}}{\hat{n}^{O}_{l}-1}\left(\mu^{O}_{i}-\overline{\mu}^{O}_{\hat{\tau}^{O}_{L,l}:\hat{\tau}^{O}_{L,l+1}}\right)^{2}}\right|\\
&+\left|\sum_{l=0}^{L}\sum_{i=\hat{\tau}^{O}_{L,l}+1}^{\hat{\tau}^{O}_{L,l+1}-1}{\frac{\hat{n}^{O}_{l}}{\hat{n}^{O}_{l}-1}\left(\mu^{O}_{i}-\overline{\mu}^{O}_{\hat{\tau}^{O}_{L,l}:\hat{\tau}^{O}_{L,l+1}}\right)^{2}}-\sum_{l=0}^{L}\sum_{i=\hat{\tau}^{O}_{L,l}+1}^{\hat{\tau}^{O}_{L,l+1}}{\left(\mu^{O}_{i}-\overline{\mu}^{O}_{\hat{\tau}^{O}_{L,l}:\hat{\tau}^{O}_{L,l+1}}\right)^{2}}\right|.\end{split}

The r.h.s. is bounded from above by

\begin{split}&2\left|\sum_{l=0}^{L}\sum_{i=\hat{\tau}^{O}_{L,l}+1}^{\hat{\tau}^{O}_{L,l+1}-1}{\frac{\hat{n}^{O}_{l}}{\hat{n}^{O}_{l}-1}\left(\mu^{E}_{i}-\overline{\mu}^{E}_{\hat{\tau}^{O}_{L,l}:\hat{\tau}^{O}_{L,l+1}-1}\right)\left(\overline{\mu}^{E}_{\hat{\tau}^{O}_{L,l}:\hat{\tau}^{O}_{L,l+1}-1}-\overline{\mu}^{O}_{\hat{\tau}^{O}_{L,l}:\hat{\tau}^{O}_{L,l+1}}\right)}\right|\\
&+\left|\sum_{l=0}^{L}\sum_{i=\hat{\tau}^{O}_{L,l}+1}^{\hat{\tau}^{O}_{L,l+1}-1}{\frac{\hat{n}^{O}_{l}}{\hat{n}^{O}_{l}-1}\left(\overline{\mu}^{E}_{\hat{\tau}^{O}_{L,l}:\hat{\tau}^{O}_{L,l+1}-1}-\overline{\mu}^{O}_{\hat{\tau}^{O}_{L,l}:\hat{\tau}^{O}_{L,l+1}}\right)^{2}}\right|\\
&+\left|\sum_{l=0}^{L}\sum_{i=\hat{\tau}^{O}_{L,l}+1}^{\hat{\tau}^{O}_{L,l+1}-1}{\frac{\hat{n}^{O}_{l}}{\hat{n}^{O}_{l}-1}\left(\mu^{E}_{i}-\mu^{O}_{i}\right)^{2}}\right|\\
&+2\left|\sum_{l=0}^{L}\sum_{i=\hat{\tau}^{O}_{L,l}+1}^{\hat{\tau}^{O}_{L,l+1}-1}{\frac{\hat{n}^{O}_{l}}{\hat{n}^{O}_{l}-1}\left(\mu^{E}_{i}-\mu^{O}_{i}\right)\left(\mu^{O}_{i}-\overline{\mu}^{O}_{\hat{\tau}^{O}_{L,l}:\hat{\tau}^{O}_{L,l+1}}\right)}\right|\\
&+\left|\sum_{l=0}^{L}\sum_{i=\hat{\tau}^{O}_{L,l}+1}^{\hat{\tau}^{O}_{L,l+1}-1}{\frac{1}{\hat{n}^{O}_{l}-1}\left(\mu^{O}_{i}-\overline{\mu}^{O}_{\hat{\tau}^{O}_{L,l}:\hat{\tau}^{O}_{L,l+1}}\right)^{2}}-\sum_{l=0}^{L}{\left(\mu^{O}_{\hat{\tau}^{O}_{L,l+1}}-\overline{\mu}^{O}_{\hat{\tau}^{O}_{L,l}:\hat{\tau}^{O}_{L,l+1}}\right)^{2}}\right|,\end{split}

which is in turn bounded from above by

\begin{split}&2\sum_{l=0}^{L}\left(\max_{i=\hat{\tau}^{O}_{L,l}+1,\ldots,\hat{\tau}^{O}_{L,l+1}-1}{\left|\mu^{E}_{i}-\overline{\mu}^{E}_{\hat{\tau}^{O}_{L,l}:\hat{\tau}^{O}_{L,l+1}-1}\right|}\right)\left(\hat{n}^{O}_{l}\left|\overline{\mu}^{E}_{\hat{\tau}^{O}_{L,l}:\hat{\tau}^{O}_{L,l+1}-1}-\overline{\mu}^{O}_{\hat{\tau}^{O}_{L,l}:\hat{\tau}^{O}_{L,l+1}}\right|\right)\\
&+\sum_{l=0}^{L}\left(\hat{n}^{O}_{l}\left|\overline{\mu}^{E}_{\hat{\tau}^{O}_{L,l}:\hat{\tau}^{O}_{L,l+1}-1}-\overline{\mu}^{O}_{\hat{\tau}^{O}_{L,l}:\hat{\tau}^{O}_{L,l+1}}\right|\right)^{2}\\
&+2\sum_{l=0}^{L}\left(\sum_{i=\hat{\tau}^{O}_{L,l}+1}^{\hat{\tau}^{O}_{L,l+1}-1}{\left|\mu^{E}_{i}-\mu^{O}_{i}\right|}\right)^{2}\\
&+4\sum_{l=0}^{L}\left(\max_{i=\hat{\tau}^{O}_{L,l}+1,\ldots,\hat{\tau}^{O}_{L,l+1}-1}{\left|\mu^{O}_{i}-\overline{\mu}^{O}_{\hat{\tau}^{O}_{L,l}:\hat{\tau}^{O}_{L,l+1}}\right|}\right)\left(\sum_{i=\hat{\tau}^{O}_{L,l}+1}^{\hat{\tau}^{O}_{L,l+1}-1}{\left|\mu^{E}_{i}-\mu^{O}_{i}\right|}\right)\\
&+\sum_{l=0}^{L}\left(\max_{i=\hat{\tau}^{O}_{L,l}+1,\ldots,\hat{\tau}^{O}_{L,l+1}-1}{\left|\mu^{O}_{i}-\overline{\mu}^{O}_{\hat{\tau}^{O}_{L,l}:\hat{\tau}^{O}_{L,l+1}}\right|}\right)^{2}\\
\leq&16B_{L}^{(n)},\end{split}

where we have used the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality and
B_{L}^{(n)}
is defined as in Lemma
15
. Then, it follows from Lemma
15
that
A_{2,L}^{(n)}
satisfies the same bounds as
A_{L}^{(n)}
in the statement of the lemma.

Bounding
A_{3,L}^{(n)}

Since

\overline{\varepsilon}^{E}_{\hat{\tau}^{O}_{L,l}:\hat{\tau}^{O}_{L,l+1}}\left(\mu^{O}_{i}-\overline{\mu}^{O}_{\hat{\tau}^{O}_{L,l}:\hat{\tau}^{O}_{L,l+1}}\right)=0=\overline{\varepsilon}^{E}_{\hat{\tau}^{O}_{L,l}:\hat{\tau}^{O}_{L,l+1}-1}\left(\mu^{O}_{i}-\overline{\mu}^{O}_{\hat{\tau}^{O}_{L,l}:\hat{\tau}^{O}_{L,l+1}}\right),

we have

\begin{split}A_{3,L}^{(n)}\leq&\Bigg|\sum_{l=0}^{L}\sum_{i=\hat{\tau}^{O}_{L,l}+1}^{\hat{\tau}^{O}_{L,l+1}-1}{\frac{\hat{n}^{O}_{l}}{\hat{n}^{O}_{l}-1}\left(\varepsilon^{E}_{i}-\overline{\varepsilon}^{E}_{\hat{\tau}^{O}_{L,l}:\hat{\tau}^{O}_{L,l+1}-1}\right)\left(\mu^{E}_{i}-\overline{\mu}^{E}_{\hat{\tau}^{O}_{L,l}:\hat{\tau}^{O}_{L,l+1}-1}\right)}\\
&-\sum_{l=0}^{L}\sum_{i=\hat{\tau}^{O}_{L,l}+1}^{\hat{\tau}^{O}_{L,l+1}-1}{\frac{\hat{n}^{O}_{l}}{\hat{n}^{O}_{l}-1}\left(\varepsilon^{E}_{i}-\overline{\varepsilon}^{E}_{\hat{\tau}^{O}_{L,l}:\hat{\tau}^{O}_{L,l+1}-1}\right)\left(\mu^{O}_{i}-\overline{\mu}^{O}_{\hat{\tau}^{O}_{L,l}:\hat{\tau}^{O}_{L,l+1}}\right)}\Bigg|\\
&+\Bigg|\sum_{l=0}^{L}\sum_{i=\hat{\tau}^{O}_{L,l}+1}^{\hat{\tau}^{O}_{L,l+1}-1}{\frac{\hat{n}^{O}_{l}}{\hat{n}^{O}_{l}-1}\left(\varepsilon^{E}_{i}-\overline{\varepsilon}^{E}_{\hat{\tau}^{O}_{L,l}:\hat{\tau}^{O}_{L,l+1}-1}\right)\left(\mu^{O}_{i}-\overline{\mu}^{O}_{\hat{\tau}^{O}_{L,l}:\hat{\tau}^{O}_{L,l+1}}\right)}\\
&-\sum_{l=0}^{L}\sum_{i=\hat{\tau}^{O}_{L,l}+1}^{\hat{\tau}^{O}_{L,l+1}}{\left(\varepsilon^{E}_{i}-\overline{\varepsilon}^{E}_{\hat{\tau}^{O}_{L,l}:\hat{\tau}^{O}_{L,l+1}-1}\right)\left(\mu^{O}_{i}-\overline{\mu}^{O}_{\hat{\tau}^{O}_{L,l}:\hat{\tau}^{O}_{L,l+1}}\right)}\Bigg|.\end{split}

Using the same arguments as for the first term gives us

\begin{split}&A_{3,L}^{(n)}\Bigg(\sum_{l=0}^{L}\Bigg(\sum_{i=\hat{\tau}^{O}_{L,l}+1}^{\hat{\tau}^{O}_{L,l+1}-1}{\left|\mu^{E}_{i}-\mu^{O}_{i}\right|}+\hat{n}^{O}_{l}\left|\overline{\mu}^{O}_{\hat{\tau}^{O}_{L,l}:\hat{\tau}^{O}_{L,l+1}}-\overline{\mu}^{E}_{\hat{\tau}^{O}_{L,l}:\hat{\tau}^{O}_{L,l+1}-1}\right|\\
&\hskip 70.0pt+\max_{i=\hat{\tau}^{O}_{L,l}+1,\ldots,\hat{\tau}^{O}_{L,l+1}}{\left|\mu^{O}_{i}-\overline{\mu}^{O}_{\hat{\tau}^{O}_{L,l}:\hat{\tau}^{O}_{L,l+1}}\right|}\Bigg)\Bigg)^{-1}\\
=&\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}}\left(\log(K_{\max})\overline{\sigma}\right).\end{split}

Hence, from Lemma
15
and the same simplifications as seen before it follows that
A_{3,L}^{(n)}
satisfies the same bounds as
A_{L}^{(n)}
in the statement of the lemma.

Bounding
A_{4,L}^{(n)}

Applying the triangle and Cauchy–Schwarz inequalities gives

\begin{split}A_{4,L}^{(n)}\leq&\left|\sum_{l=0}^{L}\sum_{i=\hat{\tau}^{O}_{L,l}+1}^{\hat{\tau}^{O}_{L,l+1}-1}{\frac{\hat{n}^{O}_{l}}{\hat{n}^{O}_{l}-1}\varepsilon^{E}_{i}\left(\overline{\mu}^{E}_{\hat{\tau}^{O}_{L,l}:\hat{\tau}^{O}_{L,l+1}-1}-\overline{\mu}^{O}_{\hat{\tau}^{O}_{L,l}:\hat{\tau}^{O}_{L,l+1}}\right)}\right|\\
&+\left|\sum_{l=0}^{L}{\hat{n}^{O}_{l}\overline{\varepsilon}^{O}_{\hat{\tau}^{O}_{L,l}:\hat{\tau}^{O}_{L,l+1}}\left(\overline{\mu}^{E}_{\hat{\tau}^{O}_{L,l}:\hat{\tau}^{O}_{L,l+1}-1}-\overline{\mu}^{O}_{\hat{\tau}^{O}_{L,l}:\hat{\tau}^{O}_{L,l+1}}\right)}\right|\\
\leq&\left|\sum_{l=0}^{L}\sum_{i=\hat{\tau}^{O}_{L,l}+1}^{\hat{\tau}^{O}_{L,l+1}-1}{\frac{\hat{n}^{O}_{l}}{\hat{n}^{O}_{l}-1}\varepsilon^{E}_{i}\left(\overline{\mu}^{E}_{\hat{\tau}^{O}_{L,l}:\hat{\tau}^{O}_{L,l+1}-1}-\overline{\mu}^{O}_{\hat{\tau}^{O}_{L,l}:\hat{\tau}^{O}_{L,l+1}}\right)}\right|\\
&+\left(\sum_{l=0}^{L}{\Big(\overline{\varepsilon}^{O}_{\hat{\tau}^{O}_{L,l}:\hat{\tau}^{O}_{L,l+1}}\Big)^{2}}\right)^{1/2}\left(\sum_{l=0}^{L}{\Big(\hat{n}^{O}_{l}\Big)^{2}\left(\overline{\mu}^{E}_{\hat{\tau}^{O}_{L,l}:\hat{\tau}^{O}_{L,l+1}-1}-\overline{\mu}^{O}_{\hat{\tau}^{O}_{L,l}:\hat{\tau}^{O}_{L,l+1}}\right)^{2}}\right)^{1/2}.\end{split}

Then it follows from Lemma
15
and the same calculations as for bounding
A_{1,L}^{(n)}
that
A_{4,L}^{(n)}
satisfies the same bounds as
A_{L}^{(n)}
in the statement of the lemma.

Bounding
A_{5,L}^{(n)}

From Lemma
15
it follows that
A_{5,L}^{(n)}
satisfies the same bounds as
A_{L}^{(n)}
in the statement of the lemma.

Proving the statement

Combining the bounds for
A_{L}^{(n)}
,
A_{1,L}^{(n)}
,
A_{2,L}^{(n)}
,
A_{3,L}^{(n)}
,
A_{4,L}^{(n)}
and
A_{5,L}^{(n)}
gives that
A_{L}^{(n)}
satisfies the bounds given in the statement of the lemma.
∎

Proof of Theorem
4
.

Recall
\operatorname{CV}^{O}_{\mathrm{mod}}(L)
,
\operatorname{CV}^{E}_{\mathrm{mod}}(L)
,
\widetilde{\operatorname{CV}}^{O}_{\mathrm{mod}}(L)
and
\widetilde{\operatorname{CV}}^{E}_{\mathrm{mod}}(L)
from (
65
) and (
66
), respectively.

Then,
\operatorname{CV}_{\mathrm{mod}}(L)=\operatorname{CV}^{O}_{\mathrm{mod}}(L)+\operatorname{CV}^{E}_{\mathrm{mod}}(L)
. Thus,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\hat{K}=K\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\min_{\begin{subarray}{c}L=0,\ldots,K_{\max},\\
L\neq K\end{subarray}}\operatorname{CV}_{\mathrm{mod}}(L)-\operatorname{CV}_{\mathrm{mod}}(K)>0\right)

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\min_{\begin{subarray}{c}L=0,\ldots,K_{\max},\\
L\neq K\end{subarray}}\operatorname{CV}^{O}_{\mathrm{mod}}(L)-\operatorname{CV}^{O}_{\mathrm{mod}}(K)>0,\min_{\begin{subarray}{c}L=0,\ldots,K_{\max},\\
L\neq K\end{subarray}}\operatorname{CV}^{E}_{\mathrm{mod}}(L)-\operatorname{CV}^{E}_{\mathrm{mod}}(K)>0\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\min_{\begin{subarray}{c}L=0,\ldots,K_{\max},\\
L\neq K\end{subarray}}\operatorname{CV}^{O}_{\mathrm{mod}}(L)-\operatorname{CV}^{O}_{\mathrm{mod}}(K)>0\right)+\mathbb{P}\left(\min_{\begin{subarray}{c}L=0,\ldots,K_{\max},\\
L\neq K\end{subarray}}\operatorname{CV}^{E}_{\mathrm{mod}}(L)-\operatorname{CV}^{E}_{\mathrm{mod}}(K)>0\right)

\displaystyle-\mathbb{P}\left(\min_{\begin{subarray}{c}L=0,\ldots,K_{\max},\\
L\neq K\end{subarray}}\operatorname{CV}^{O}_{\mathrm{mod}}(L)-\operatorname{CV}^{O}_{\mathrm{mod}}(K)>0\text{ or }\min_{\begin{subarray}{c}L=0,\ldots,K_{\max},\\
L\neq K\end{subarray}}\operatorname{CV}^{E}_{\mathrm{mod}}(L)-\operatorname{CV}^{E}_{\mathrm{mod}}(K)>0\right).

In the following we will show that

\mathbb{P}\Bigg(\min_{\begin{subarray}{c}L=0,\ldots,K_{\max},\\
L\neq K\end{subarray}}\operatorname{CV}^{O}_{\mathrm{mod}}(L)-\operatorname{CV}^{O}_{\mathrm{mod}}(K)>0\Bigg)\to 1,\text{ as }n\to\infty.

(98)

Note that this completes the proof as it implies

\mathbb{P}\Bigg(\min_{\begin{subarray}{c}L=0,\ldots,K_{\max},\\
L\neq K\end{subarray}}\operatorname{CV}^{E}_{\mathrm{mod}}(L)-\operatorname{CV}^{E}_{\mathrm{mod}}(K)>0\Bigg)\to 1,\text{ as }n\to\infty,

since
\operatorname{CV}^{O}_{\mathrm{mod}}(L)
and
\operatorname{CV}^{E}_{\mathrm{mod}}(L)
are symmetric and for both terms the same properties follow from Assumptions
1
–
5
. In fact, one way to see this is to consider the observations in reverse order, which interchanges
Y^{O}_{i}
and
Y^{E}_{i}
, but Assumptions
1
–
5
are not altered. Hence, (
98
) implies
\mathbb{P}\left(\hat{K}=K\right)\to 1,\text{ as }n\to\infty
.

It remains to show (
98
). We will consider
L>K
and
L<K
separately. Let
L>K
. Recall the notation
A_{L}^{(n)}=\left|\operatorname{CV}^{O}_{\mathrm{mod}}(L)-\widetilde{\operatorname{CV}}^{O}_{\mathrm{mod}}(L)\right|

from Lemma
16
. It follows from Lemmas
14
and
16
that

\displaystyle\min_{L=K+1,\ldots,K_{\max}}\operatorname{CV}^{O}_{\mathrm{mod}}(L)-\operatorname{CV}^{O}_{\mathrm{mod}}(K)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\min_{L=K+1,\ldots,K_{\max}}\left\{\widetilde{\operatorname{CV}}^{O}_{\mathrm{mod}}(L)-A_{L}^{(n)}\right\}-\widetilde{\operatorname{CV}}^{O}_{\mathrm{mod}}(K)+A_{K}^{(n)}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\min_{L=K+1,\ldots,K_{\max}}\left\{S_{\varepsilon^{O}}\left(\mathcal{T}^{O}_{K}\right)-S_{\varepsilon^{O}}\left(\hat{\mathcal{T}}^{O}_{L}\cup\mathcal{T}^{O}_{K}\right)\right\}\left(1+o_{\mathbb{P}}(1)\right).

It follows from Assumption
4
that the r.h.s. is positive with probability converging to
1
as
n\to\infty
. Hence, (
98
) holds when
L>K
. Note that thus far, Assumptions
3
and
5
have not been used directly, and moreover they are not required for the referenced Lemmas when
K=0
for all
n
.

Let
L<K
now. Lemma
15
yields that there exist a constant
A>0
and sequences of stochastic non-empty sets
\mathcal{I}_{L}\subseteq\{1,\ldots,K\}
such that

\mathbb{P}\Bigg(\forall\,L<K,\,\,\sum_{i=\tau_{k}^{O}-\lfloor\frac{\underline{\lambda}}{4}\rfloor+1}^{\tau_{k}^{O}+\lfloor\frac{\underline{\lambda}}{4}\rfloor}{\big(\mu^{O}_{i}-\overline{\mu}^{O}_{L,i}\big)^{2}}\geq A\underline{\lambda}\Delta_{k}^{2}\ \forall\ k\in\mathcal{I}_{L}\Bigg)\to 1,

where
\overline{\mu}^{O}_{L,i}:=\sum_{l=0}^{L}{\mathbb{1}_{\{\hat{\tau}^{O}_{L,l}+1\leq i\leq\hat{\tau}^{O}_{L,l+1}\}}\overline{\mu}^{O}_{\hat{\tau}^{O}_{L,l}:\hat{\tau}^{O}_{L,l+1}}}
. Thus, it follows from Lemmas
14
and
16
that

\displaystyle\min_{L=0\ldots,K-1}\operatorname{CV}^{O}_{\mathrm{mod}}(L)-\operatorname{CV}^{O}_{\mathrm{mod}}(K)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\min_{L=0\ldots,K-1}\left\{\widetilde{\operatorname{CV}}^{O}_{\mathrm{mod}}(L)-A_{L}^{(n)}\right\}-\widetilde{\operatorname{CV}}^{O}_{\mathrm{mod}}(K)+A_{K}^{(n)}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\min_{L=0\ldots,K-1}\Bigg\{\underline{\lambda}\sum_{k\in\mathcal{I}_{L}}{\Delta_{k}^{2}}\Bigg\}\big(A+o_{\mathbb{P}}(1)\big).

The r.h.s. is positive, since
|\mathcal{I}_{L}|\geq 1,\ \forall L=L=0\ldots,K-1
and
A>0
. Hence, (
98
) holds also when
L<K
. This completes the proof as noted before.
∎

S6
Proof of Theorem
3

The first statement in Theorem
3
follows directly from Theorem
4
if its assumptions are satisfied by least squares estimation under the given setting. To show this, we will use Theorem
5
and Lemma
17
. The latter is a shorter version of Theorem 2 in
zou2020consistent
. It shows that Assumption
4
is indeed satisfied in the given set-up.

Lemma 17
.

Suppose the noise assumption in Section
3.2
, i.e. sub-Gaussian noise, constant variance on each segment and ratio between the smallest variance and largest variance proxy is bounded from below. Then, for all
M>0
,

\mathbb{P}\left(\frac{\min_{L=K+1,\ldots,K_{\max}}\left\{S_{\varepsilon^{O}}\left(\mathcal{T}^{O}_{K}\right)-S_{\varepsilon^{O}}\left(\hat{\mathcal{T}}^{O}_{L}\cup\mathcal{T}^{O}_{K}\right)\right\}}{\overline{\sigma}^{2}\log\log\overline{\lambda}}<M\right)\to 0

and as above but with all instances of
O
replaced by
E
.

Proof.

Let
{k^{*}}
be the index of the longest segment, so
\overline{\lambda}/2-1\leq\tau^{O}_{{k^{*}}+1}-\tau^{O}_{k^{*}}\leq\overline{\lambda}/2+1
. Then, it follows from the fact that adding change-points only decreases the costs and the definition of least squares estimation that for every
L=K+1,\ldots,K_{\max}
,

\displaystyle S_{\varepsilon^{O}}\left(\hat{\mathcal{T}}^{O}_{L}\cup\mathcal{T}^{O}_{K}\right)=

\displaystyle S_{Y^{O}}\left(\hat{\mathcal{T}}^{O}_{L}\cup\mathcal{T}^{O}_{K}\right)\leq S_{Y^{O}}\left(\hat{\mathcal{T}}^{O}_{K+1}\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\min_{\tau^{O}_{k^{*}}<t<\tau^{O}_{{k^{*}}+1}}S_{Y^{O}}\left(\mathcal{T}^{O}_{K}\cup\{t\}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\min_{\tau^{O}_{k^{*}}<t<\tau^{O}_{{k^{*}}+1}}S_{\varepsilon^{O}}\left(\mathcal{T}^{O}_{K}\cup\{t\}\right).

Thus,

\displaystyle S_{\varepsilon^{O}}\left(\mathcal{T}^{O}_{K}\right)-S_{\varepsilon^{O}}\left(\hat{\mathcal{T}}^{O}_{L}\cup\mathcal{T}^{O}_{K}\right)

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle S_{\varepsilon^{O}}\left(\mathcal{T}^{O}_{K}\right)-\min_{\tau^{O}_{k^{*}}<t<\tau^{O}_{{k^{*}}+1}}S_{\varepsilon^{O}}\left(\mathcal{T}^{O}_{K}\cup\{t\}\right)

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\max_{\tau^{O}_{k^{*}}<t<\tau^{O}_{{k^{*}}+1}}\left\{(t-\tau^{O}_{k^{*}})\left(\overline{\varepsilon}^{O}_{\tau^{O}_{k^{*}}:t}\right)^{2}+(\tau^{O}_{{k^{*}}+1}-t)\left(\overline{\varepsilon}^{O}_{t:\tau^{O}_{{k^{*}}+1}}\right)^{2}\right\}-(\tau^{O}_{{k^{*}}+1}-\tau^{O}_{k^{*}})\left(\overline{\varepsilon}^{O}_{\tau^{O}_{k^{*}}:\tau^{O}_{{k^{*}}+1}}\right)^{2}.

It follows from
zou2020consistent
(see also
horvath1993maximum
) that there exists a constant
c>0
such that

\mathbb{P}\left(\max_{\tau^{O}_{k^{*}}<t<\tau^{O}_{{k^{*}}+1}}\left\{(t-\tau^{O}_{k^{*}})\left(\overline{\varepsilon}^{O}_{\tau^{O}_{k^{*}}:t}\right)^{2}+(\tau^{O}_{{k^{*}}+1}-t)\left(\overline{\varepsilon}^{O}_{t:\tau^{O}_{{k^{*}}+1}}\right)^{2}\right\}>c\,\underline{\sigma}^{2}\log\log\overline{\lambda}\right)\to 1.

Moreover,
(\tau^{O}_{{k^{*}}+1}-\tau^{O}_{k^{*}})\Big(\overline{\varepsilon}^{O}_{\tau^{O}_{k^{*}}:\tau^{O}_{{k^{*}}+1}}\Big)^{2}=\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}}(\sigma)
. Hence, the stated formula follows by using that
\limsup_{n\to\infty}\sigma/\underline{\sigma}<\infty
.

The same argument holds with all instances of
O
replaced by
E
.
∎

Proof of Theorem
3
.

For the first part we will use Theorem
4
and hence, we first verify its assumptions in the following. Note that Assumptions
1
and
5
are assumed in Theorem
3
as well. Since
\varepsilon_{1},\ldots,\varepsilon_{n}
are sub-Gaussian with uniformly bounded variance proxy
\sigma^{2}
, Assumption
2
follows. Moreover, Lemma
17
shows that Assumption
4
holds as well. It remains to show Assumption
3
. To this end, we will apply Theorem
5
to
Y_{1}^{O},\ldots,Y_{n/2}^{O}
. Note that this sequence has
K
change-points with minimal jump size
\Delta_{(1)}
, minimal distance between change-points of at least
\lfloor\,\underline{\lambda}/2\rfloor
, and maximal distance between change-points of at most
\overline{\lambda}/2
. As noted in the discussion following Theorem
4
, Assumption
3
is not required for the conclusion when
K=0
. Hence, we can assume that
K>0
.

Now let
C_{n}
be a sequence with
C_{n}\to\infty
but such that

\displaystyle C_{n}^{2}K\log\log\bigg((\log(K)\vee 1)\frac{\sigma^{2}}{\Delta_{(1)}^{2}}\vee e\bigg)

\displaystyle=o(\log\log\overline{\lambda}),

(99)

\displaystyle C_{n}K(\log K\vee 1)

\displaystyle=o(\log\log\overline{\lambda}).

(100)

The existence of such a sequence is guaranteed by (
17
) and Assumption
1
(ii)
. Now we set

\displaystyle\gamma_{0k}

\displaystyle:=C_{n}\left(\log\left(K\right)\vee 1\right)\frac{\sigma^{2}}{\Delta_{k}^{2}},

\displaystyle\gamma_{1k}

\displaystyle:=C_{n}\left(\log\left(K\frac{\sigma^{2}}{\Delta_{k}^{2}}\right)\vee 1\right)\frac{\sigma^{2}}{\Delta_{k}^{2}}.

This choice satisfies (
24
). We now claim that taking
\delta_{0k}=\lfloor\gamma_{0k}\rfloor
and
\delta_{1k}=\lfloor\gamma_{1k}\rfloor
satisfies Assumption
3
. It follows from (
25
) in Theorem
5
that Assumption
3
(i)
is satisfied, if we show that
\gamma_{0,k}\vee\gamma_{1,k}\leq\underline{\lambda}/2-1
. It follows from (
17
) that

\displaystyle K\frac{\sigma^{2}}{\Delta_{k}^{2}}=o(\underline{\lambda}).

Thus, (
100
) and (
17
) imply

\displaystyle\frac{\gamma_{0,k}\vee\gamma_{1,k}}{\underline{\lambda}}\leq o\left(\frac{\sigma^{2}}{\underline{\lambda}\Delta_{k}^{2}}\log(\underline{\lambda})\log\log\overline{\lambda}\right)\to 0.

From (
99
), we see that Assumption
3
(ii)
is satisfied, since

\displaystyle\max_{q=0,1;\ 1\leq k\leq K}K\log\log{(\delta_{q,k}\vee e)}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle K\log\log\left(C_{n}\left(\log\left(K\frac{\sigma^{2}}{\Delta_{k}^{2}}\right)\vee(\log K)\vee 1\right)\frac{\sigma^{2}}{\Delta_{k}^{2}}\vee e\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathcal{O}\left(C_{n}K\log\log\left(\left(\log(K)\vee 1\right)\frac{\sigma^{2}}{\Delta_{(1)}^{2}}\vee e\right)\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle o(\log\log\overline{\lambda}).

Next, from (
100
) we see that Assumption
3
(iii)
is satisfied, since

\sum_{k=1}^{K}\delta_{0,k}\Delta_{k}^{2}\leq C_{n}K(\log(K)\vee 1)\sigma^{2}=o(\sigma^{2}\log\log\overline{\lambda}).

Furthermore, Assumption
3
(iv)
is satisfied as well, since if
\frac{\sigma^{2}\log\log\overline{\lambda}}{K\Delta_{k}^{2}}\leq C_{n}
, then

\displaystyle\gamma_{0,k}\vee\gamma_{1,k}\leq\frac{C_{n}^{2}K}{\log\log\overline{\lambda}}\left\{\log K\vee\log\left(\frac{C_{n}}{\log\log\overline{\lambda}}\right)\vee 1\right\}\vee 1\to 0,

due to (
100
). Thus,
\delta_{0k}=\lfloor\gamma_{0,k}\rfloor=\delta_{1k}=\lfloor\gamma_{1,k}\rfloor=0
.
Finally, it follows from
\delta_{1k}=0
if
\frac{\sigma^{2}\log\log\overline{\lambda}}{K\Delta_{k}^{2}}\leq C_{n}
and from (
26
) in Theorem
5
that Assumption
3
(v)
is satisfied as well. To this end, recall that the minimal distance between two change-points is at least
\lfloor\,\underline{\lambda}/2\rfloor
and note that

⌊

⌊

λ

2

⌋

2

⌋= ⌊

λ

4

⌋.

Hence, Assumption
3
holds. The same is true when we replace O’s by E’s. Consequently, it follows from Theorem
4
that
\mathbb{P}\left(\hat{K}=K\right)\to 1,\text{ as }n\to\infty
.

It remains to show (
18
). Let us write
\hat{\tau}_{k}:=\hat{\tau}_{K,k}
for
k=0,\ldots,K+1
and
\hat{\delta}_{k}:=|\hat{\tau}_{k}-\tau_{k}|
. Recall that
\hat{f}_{K}:\ [0,1]\to\mathbb{R},\ t\mapsto\sum_{k=0}^{K}{\overline{Y}_{\hat{\tau}_{k}:\hat{\tau}_{k+1}}\mathbb{1}_{(\hat{\tau}_{k}/n,\hat{\tau}_{k+1}/n]}(t)}
. We have that

\begin{split}&n\int_{0}^{1}{\left(\hat{f}_{K}(t)-f(t)\right)^{2}dt}\\
\leq&\sum_{k=0}^{K}(\hat{\tau}_{k+1}-\hat{\tau}_{k})|\overline{Y}_{\hat{\tau}_{k}:\hat{\tau}_{k+1}}-\beta_{k}|^{2}+\sum_{k=1}^{K}\hat{\delta}_{k}\left(\Delta_{k}+\max\big(|\overline{Y}_{\hat{\tau}_{k-1}:\hat{\tau}_{k}}-\beta_{k-1}|,\,|\overline{Y}_{\hat{\tau}_{k}:\hat{\tau}_{k+1}}-\beta_{k}|\big)\right)^{2}.\end{split}

In the following we will bound these terms. It follows from Theorem
5
and the fact that
\mathbb{P}\left(\hat{K}=K\right)\to 1
, that for
\gamma_{k}:=c_{n}(\log(K)\vee 1)\sigma^{2}/\Delta_{k}^{2}
, where
c_{n}
can be any sequence such that
c_{n}\to\infty
, as
n\to\infty
,

\mathbb{P}\left(\hat{\delta}_{k}\leq\gamma_{k}\ \forall\ k=1,\ldots,K\text{ and }\hat{K}=K\right)\to 1.

(101)

Furthermore, (
32
) implies
\gamma_{k}<\underline{\lambda}/2
for a suitable chosen
c_{n}
. In the following, we work on the sequence of events in (
101
). Consequently,
\hat{\tau}_{k+1}-\hat{\tau}_{k}\geq\underline{\lambda}/2
.

We also have that

|\overline{Y}_{\hat{\tau}_{k}:\hat{\tau}_{k+1}}-\beta_{k}|\leq\frac{\hat{\delta}_{k}\Delta_{k}+\hat{\delta}_{k+1}\Delta_{k+1}}{\hat{\tau}_{k+1}-\hat{\tau}_{k}}+|\overline{\varepsilon}_{\hat{\tau}_{k}:\hat{\tau}_{k+1}}|.

For the following calculation we assume w.l.o.g. that
\hat{\tau}_{k}<\tau_{k}<\tau_{k+1}<\hat{\tau}_{k+1}
, since other cases lead to the same bound. Then,

\begin{split}&(\hat{\tau}_{k+1}-\hat{\tau}_{k})|\overline{\varepsilon}_{\hat{\tau}_{k}:\hat{\tau}_{k+1}}|^{2}\\
\leq&3\left((\tau_{k}-\hat{\tau}_{k})|\overline{\varepsilon}_{\hat{\tau}_{k}:\tau_{k}}|^{2}+(\tau_{k+1}-\tau_{k})|\overline{\varepsilon}_{\tau_{k}:\tau_{k+1}}|^{2}+(\hat{\tau}_{k+1}-\tau_{k+1})|\overline{\varepsilon}_{\tau_{k+1}:\hat{\tau}_{k+1}}|^{2}\right)\\
\leq&\max_{t=\tau_{k}-\gamma_{k},\ldots,\tau_{k}-1}\{(\tau_{k}-t)|\overline{\varepsilon}_{t:\tau_{k}}|^{2}\}+(\tau_{k+1}-\tau_{k})|\overline{\varepsilon}_{\tau_{k}:\tau_{k+1}}|^{2}\\
&\hskip 15.0pt+\max_{t=\tau_{k+1}+1,\ldots,\tau_{k+1}+\gamma_{k+1}}\{(t-\tau_{k+1})|\overline{\varepsilon}_{\tau_{k+1}:t}|^{2}\}.\end{split}

Hence, due to sub-Gaussianity and independence,

\max_{k=1,\ldots,K}\big(\log\log\gamma_{k}+1+\log\log\gamma_{k+1}\big)^{-1}(\hat{\tau}_{k+1}-\hat{\tau}_{k})|\overline{\varepsilon}_{\hat{\tau}_{k}:\hat{\tau}_{k+1}}|^{2}=\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}}(\log(K)\sigma^{2}).

Note that here and in the following we have written
\log\log\gamma_{k}
instead of
\log\log(\gamma_{k}\vee e)
to improve readability.

Thus, using
(x+y)^{2}\leq 2x^{2}+2y^{2}
,

\begin{split}&\sum_{k=0}^{K}(\hat{\tau}_{k+1}-\hat{\tau}_{k})|\overline{Y}_{\hat{\tau}_{k}:\hat{\tau}_{k+1}}-\beta_{k}|^{2}=\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}}\left(\sum_{k=1}^{K}\frac{\gamma_{k}\Delta_{k}^{2}}{\underline{\lambda}}\right)+\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}}\left(\sum_{k=1}^{K}(1+\log\log\gamma_{k})\log(K)\sigma^{2}\right),\end{split}

and

\begin{split}&\sum_{k=1}^{K}\hat{\delta}_{k}\left(\Delta_{k}+\max\big(|\overline{Y}_{\hat{\tau}_{k-1}:\hat{\tau}_{k}}-\beta_{k-1}|,\,|\overline{Y}_{\hat{\tau}_{k}:\hat{\tau}_{k+1}}-\beta_{k}|\big)\right)^{2}\\
=&\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}}\left(\sum_{k=1}^{K}\gamma_{k}\Delta_{k}^{2}\right)+\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}}\left(\sum_{k=1}^{K}\gamma_{k}\frac{\left(\gamma_{k-1}^{2}\Delta_{k-1}^{2}+\gamma_{k}^{2}\Delta_{k}^{2}+\gamma_{k+1}^{2}\Delta_{k+1}^{2}\right)}{\underline{\lambda}^{2}}\right)\\
&+\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}}\left(\sum_{k=1}^{K}\gamma_{k}(1+\log\log\gamma_{k-1}+\log\log\gamma_{k}+\log\log\gamma_{k+1})\log(K)\sigma^{2}/\underline{\lambda}\right),\end{split}

where we have used the notation
\Delta_{0}=\Delta_{K+1}=0
and
\gamma_{0}=\gamma_{K+1}=e
.

Since
\gamma_{k}\leq\underline{\lambda}
, it follows that

\begin{split}&n\int_{0}^{1}{\left(\hat{f}_{K}(t)-f(t)\right)^{2}dt}\\
=&\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}}\left(\sum_{k=1}^{K}\gamma_{k}\Delta_{k}^{2}\right)+\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}}\left(\sum_{k=1}^{K}(1+\log\log\gamma_{k})\log(K)\sigma^{2}\right)\\
=&\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}}\left(\sum_{k=1}^{K}(c_{n}+\log\log\gamma_{k})(\log(K)\vee 1)\sigma^{2}\right)\\
=&\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}}\left(\left(c_{n}+\log\log\left(c_{n}(\log(K)\vee 1)\sigma^{2}/\Delta_{(1)}^{2}\right)\right)K(\log(K)\vee 1)\sigma^{2}\right).\end{split}

By using the second part of (
17
) and that
\log\log\overline{\lambda}\to\infty
, as
n\to\infty
, as well as by choosing a
c_{n}
that increases slowly enough, this simplifies to the claimed bound.
∎
